## Review

**7 out of 10, Effective.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

This is a reading document for investors, and it works as one: the contents page (2) gives a route, the highlights page (3) groups figures under Profitability, Cash and Operations, and the financial and operational tables (4 to 7) share one consistent layout with the latest quarter shaded. Photo pages (15 to 23) each carry one image with a short caption, and the statements and reconciliations (28 to 33) keep a repeatable structure. The weaknesses are real, though: no page states a takeaway as a headline, footnotes and small table text are very faint and tiny, and slides 8, 10, 11 and 12 are dense paragraphs. Slide 3 puts grey text on a mid-grey ground, and the shaded Q4 column on the tables is the only emphasis, so the reader must find the story themselves.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the grouped highlights structure on slide 3, the shaded current-period column and consistent table grid on slides 4 to 7, and the one-image-per-page photo section with short captions.

**Most useful repair:** Raise the contrast on slide 3 and on the pale footnotes, and add a plain-language headline to the text-heavy narrative pages (8, 10, 11, 12) so the main point comes first.

**What would change the score:** I would add short takeaway titles to the financial summary and key metrics pages (4, 5, 24, 25) and break the long paragraphs into scannable bullets or paired text and visual.

_Confidence medium. 35 of 35 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck opens with a Summary page of highlights covering profitability, cash and operations, followed by a short letter-style narrative on the year. It then moves through financial and operational summaries, manufacturing and hardware capacity, supporting infrastructure, AI and software, services, other updates and an outlook. Photo and chart pages sit in the middle, followed by key metrics charts, detailed financial statements, reconciliations of GAAP to non-GAAP measures, and additional information with a webcast notice and forward-looking statement disclaimers. The deck is data-dense and table-heavy, with quarterly and annual comparisons side by side. Slides worth studying include the Summary highlights page, which pairs bolded headline figures with short labels, the Financial Summary tables with year-over-year columns, and the Outlook page, which organises forward commentary under short headings such as Volume, Cash and Profit. Several slides use a single large image or chart to carry the point. The closing sections keep reconciliations in a consistent layout so readers can trace each non-GAAP figure back to its GAAP source.

## Questions about this deck

**What does the Tesla deck say about its 2025 profitability?**

Slide 3 reports $4.4B in GAAP operating income in 2025, $3.8B in GAAP net income, and $5.9B in non-GAAP net income. The same slide gives Q4 figures of $1.4B, $0.8B, and $1.8B for those measures.

**How many slides are in the Tesla Q4 and FY 2025 update deck?**

The Tesla deck has 35 pages in total. Slide 2 lists its contents, from the Highlights on slide 3 through to Additional Information on slide 34.

**What is Tesla's outlook on volume production for Cybercab, Tesla Semi and Megapack 3?**

Slide 13 says Cybercab, Tesla Semi and Megapack 3 are on schedule for volume production starting in 2026. The same slide says the company is focused on maximum capacity utilization at its factories.

**How is the Tesla deck structured, and where do the financial statements start?**

Slide 2 gives the structure: Highlights, Financial Summary, Operational Summary, Manufacturing & Hardware, Supporting Infrastructure, AI & Software, Services, Other Updates, Outlook, Photos & Charts, Key Metrics, Financial Statements, and Additional Information. Financial Statements begin on slide 27.

### Slide 3

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Headline figures are set in bold with short labels grouped under Profitability, Cash and Operations, so the key numbers scan quickly.
- Review: Slide 3 puts grey text on a mid-grey ground, and the shaded Q4 column on the tables is the only emphasis, so the reader must find the story themselves.
- Worth keeping: Keep the grouped highlights structure on slide 3, the shaded current-period column and consistent table grid on slides 4 to 7, and the one-image-per-page photo section with short captions.
- Suggested repair: Raise the contrast on slide 3 and on the pale footnotes, and add a plain-language headline to the text-heavy narrative pages (8, 10, 11, 12) so the main point comes first.

**Text**

HIGHLIGHTS
SUMMARY
Profitability
$4.4B GAAP operating income in 2025; $1.4B in Q4
$3.8B GAAP net income in 2025; $0.8B in Q4
$5.9B non-GAAP net income¹ in 2025; $1.8B in Q4
2025 marked a critical year for Tesla as we further expanded our mission and continued our transition from a hardware-centric business to a physical AI company. We laid the foundation for the future of Tesla as we further advanced FSD (Supervised)⁴, launched our Robotaxi service, began installing production lines for Cybercab and fine-tuned our production-primed Optimus design while expanding our AI training infrastructure.
Our approach to autonomous vehicles and humanoid robots mirrors the way we approached electric vehicles and energy storage – at the system level where we identify the limiting factor and develop bespoke and scalable solutions (batteries, power electronics, inverters, software, AI silicon, etc.) to optimize for cost, functionality, efficiency and safety. Our vertical integration has enabled us to achieve economies of scale in a profitable manner, quickly troubleshoot bottlenecks in production and iteratively optimize our technologies more rapidly than others.
Cash
Operating cash flow of $14.7B in 2025; $3.8B in Q4
Free cash flow² of $6.2B in 2025; $1.4B in Q4
$7.5B increase in our cash and investments³ in 2025 to $44.1B
In 2025, we completed the refresh of our vehicle lineup with the launch of the new Model Y, including additional variants. We believe that maintaining an optimized and efficient product portfolio, with a continued focus on high-value features such as long range, best-in-class software and autonomy, is the correct strategy to win the autos market of the future. Similarly, we continued to evolve our energy offerings for commercial, utility and retail customers, as we position ourselves as a supplier of choice for clean, affordable and rapidly deployable energy capacity ahead of expected sustained demand growth for electricity.
Operations
Began removing safety monitor from our Robotaxis in Austin in January
Record Q4 & FY’25 energy storage deployments
Record vehicle deliveries in APAC
In 2026, we will further invest in the infrastructure needed to support clean energy and transport and autonomous robots, including the ramp of six new production lines across vehicle, robots, energy storage and battery manufacturing, while further leveraging our existing factory, charging and service center footprint to support future growth.
3
(1) Excludes SBC (stock-based compensation) & Digital assets gains and losses, net of tax; (2) Free cash flow = operating cash flow less capex; (3) Includes cash, cash equivalents and investments; (4) Active driver supervision required; does not make the vehicle autonomous; Note: all information herein refers to the current quarter unless otherwise noted.
TESLA

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: The quarterly financial summary places five quarters and a YoY column side by side, making trends readable at a glance.
- Worth keeping: Keep the grouped highlights structure on slide 3, the shaded current-period column and consistent table grid on slides 4 to 7, and the one-image-per-page photo section with short captions.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no gradient. Title 'FINANCIAL SUMMARY' in dark grey #333333, small wide-tracked capitals, about 2.0% of slide height, Calibri regular. Table text is black #000000 Calibri regular at about 2.0% of slide height, roughly 1.5% for the small superscripts. Table header is a mid-grey #7F7F7F band with white #FFFFFF text. Body rows alternate light grey #F2F2F2 and white with no visible rules. The 2025 column uses a pale rose tint, approximately #F2DCDB. Footnotes are light grey #BFBFBF Calibri at about 1.6% of slide height; page number is #BABABA. Corners are square, no shadows.

**Layout**

Single full-width data table on a white field. Section title and '(Unaudited)' sit top-left at about 9% of slide height, with the table starting at 14% and filling the width from about 4.6% to 95.3% margins. Footnotes sit in the bottom band from about 89% down, left-aligned at 5%; the page number '6' sits bottom-left at about 95% and a faint Tesla wordmark sits bottom-right. No slide-level logo or footer beyond these.

**Visuals**

Table. Columns: row label (unnamed, wide, about 4.6% to 43.5% of width), Q4-2024, Q1-2025, Q2-2025, Q3-2025, Q4-2025 (highlighted column), YoY. Rows: Model 3/Y production 436,718 / 345,454 / 396,835 / 435,826 / 422,652 / -3%; Other models production 22,727 / 17,161 / 13,409 / 11,624 / 11,706 / -48%; Total production (bold) 459,445 / 362,615 / 410,244 / 447,450 / 434,358 / -5%; blank spacer row; Model 3/Y deliveries 471,930 / 323,800 / 373,728 / 481,166 / 406,585 / -14%; Other models deliveries 23,640 / 12,881 / 10,394 / 15,933 / 11,642 / -51%; Total deliveries (bold) 495,570 / 336,681 / 384,122 / 497,099 / 418,227 / -16%; of which subject to operating lease accounting 26,962 / 13,721 / 6,670 / 10,230 / 10,996 / -59%; blank spacer; Cumulative deliveries (all-time; mil) 7.3 / 7.6 / 8.0 / 8.5 / 8.9 / 22%; Active FSD Subscriptions (mil) 0.8 / 0.8 / 0.9 / 1.0 / 1.1 / 38%; blank spacer; Total end of quarter operating lease (new vehicle) count 180,523 / 179,930 / 172,882 / 167,163 / 163,075 / -10%; Global vehicle inventory (days of supply) 12 / 22 / 24 / 10 / 15 / 25%; blank spacer; Storage deployed (GWh) 11.0 / 10.4 / 9.6 / 12.5 / 14.2 / 29%; blank spacer; Tesla locations 1,359 / 1,390 / 1,454 / 1,498 / 1,553 / 14%; blank spacer; Supercharger stations 6,975 / 7,131 / 7,377 / 7,753 / 8,182 / 17%; Supercharger connectors 65,495 / 67,316 / 70,228 / 73,817 / 77,682 / 19%. The Q4-2025 column cells carry a pale rose tint. No chart, photograph or icon.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean corporate financial table. Background pure white #FFFFFF. Section title is widely letter-spaced regular Calibri-style sans-serif in dark grey #333333, about 2% of slide height for caps. Table header row is a solid mid-grey bar #7F7F7F with white #FFFFFF regular sans-serif labels. Body rows alternate light grey #F2F2F2 bands with white gaps between rows; body text is black #000000 Calibri regular, about 2% of slide height, with totals in Calibri Bold. The Q4-2025 column is tinted pale rose about #F2DCDB. Footnotes are very light grey #BFBFBF Calibri about 1.6% of height with small superscript numerals. Page number is light grey #BABABA. No shadows, gradients, rounded corners or borders; table cells have hairline white separators.

**Layout**

A single full-width data table on a white page with the section title and '(Unaudited)' label top-left above the table, which runs from about 4.6% to 95.4% of slide width and from about 14% to 81.5% of slide height. Footnotes sit in small light grey text in the lower-left, the page number '7' sits bottom-left below them, and a faint wordmark sits bottom-right; margins are about 4.6% left and right.

**Visuals**

Table with 7 columns: an unlabelled row-label column (about 38.7% of slide width), then 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024, 2025 and YoY columns. Header row is a dark grey band (#7F7F7F) with white centred year labels. Rows, in order, with values as printed: (1) Model 3/Y production: 906,032 | 1,298,434 | 1,775,159 | 1,679,338 | 1,600,767 | -5%. (2) Other models production: 24,390 | 71,177 | 70,826 | 94,105 | 53,900 | -43%. (3) Total production (bold): 930,422 | 1,369,611 | 1,845,985 | 1,773,443 | 1,654,667 | -7%. (blank spacer row). (4) Model 3/Y deliveries: 911,242 | 1,247,146 | 1,739,707 | 1,704,093 | 1,585,279 | -7%. (5) Other models deliveries: 24,980 | 66,705 | 68,874 | 85,133 | 50,850 | -40%. (6) Total deliveries (bold): 936,222 | 1,313,851 | 1,808,581 | 1,789,226 | 1,636,129 | -9%. (8) Cumulative deliveries (1) (all-time; mil): 2.3 | 3.7 | 5.5 | 7.3 | 8.9 | 22%. (9) Active FSD Subscriptions (2) (mil): 0.4 | 0.5 | 0.6 | 0.8 | 1.1 | 38%. (blank spacer). (10) Total end of year operating lease (new vehicle) count: 120,342 | 140,667 | 176,564 | 180,523 | 163,075 | -10%. (11) Global vehicle inventory (days of supply) (3): 6 | 16 | 16 | 13 | 15 | 15%. The 2025 column cells carry a pale pink tint (about #F2DCDB) to highlight them. No chart, photograph or icon is present. Superscript markers (1), (2) and (3) appear after the row labels. **Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain corporate financial-summary style on white. Section title is set in letterspaced capitals in dark grey #333333. Body text is black #000000 in a sans-serif (Calibri) at regular weight, with bold for the total rows. Header row uses #7F7F7F with white text. Alternating row bands are #F2F2F2 with white gaps between bands. Highlighted 2025 cells use a pale pink tint. Footnotes are very light grey #BFBFBF. Title and body text are both very small, about 2.0% of slide height. Rectangular shapes with no corner radius, no shadows and no gradients.

Automotive
While automotive sales declined sequentially, gross margin (even when excluding the impact of regulatory credits) improved. The APAC region continued to show strength across multiple markets and set a record for deliveries in the quarter. We continued the rollout of Model Y variants across markets in Q4, including the standard and performance versions.
Preparations continue in North America for the production ramps of Tesla Semi and Cybercab, both commencing 1H26, and production of the next-generation Roadster.
Energy generation and storage
We achieved our highest quarterly energy storage deployments, driven by record Megapack deployments. Total gross profit rose, both sequentially and year-over-year, to a record $1.1 billion, marking the fifth consecutive record quarter. We plan to begin Megapack 3 and Megablock production at Megafactory Houston in 2026. In 2025, our global Powerwall network supported more than 89,000 Virtual Power Plant events across over 1 million installed units, allowing homeowners to save over $1 billion in electricity bills as Virtual Power Plant participation continues to scale rapidly.
Robotics
We made further progress on the Optimus program in 2025. In Q1 of this year, we plan to unveil the Gen 3 version of Optimus, which will include major upgrades from version 2.5, including our latest hand design. The Gen 3 is our first design meant for mass production. Preparations are underway for the first production line, including supply chain readiness, with start of production planned before the end of 2026 and eventual planned capacity of 1 million robots per year.
Installed capacity ≠ current production rate and there may be limitations discovered as production rates approach capacity. Production rates depend on a variety of factors, including equipment uptime, component supply, downtime related to factory upgrades, regulatory considerations and other factors. Construction includes factory and infrastructure buildout as well as tool installation.
8

The section label MANUFACTURING & HARDWARE sits top-left at about 8.9% from the top with a left margin of about 4.6%. The page number 8 sits bottom-left at about 95% from the top. A faint wordmark sits bottom-right. Left and right margins are about 4.5%.

**Visuals**

Table titled Installed Annual Manufacturing Capacity with four columns (Region, Product, Capacity, Status) and four section rows (Automotive, Energy Generation and Storage, Robotics; Automotive appears as the first section row). Automotive rows: California / Model 3 / Model Y / >550,000 / Production; blank region / Model S / Model X / 100,000 / Production; Shanghai / Model 3 / Model Y / >950,000 / Production; Berlin / Model Y / >375,000 / Production; Texas / Model Y / >250,000 / Production; blank / Cybertruck / >125,000 / Production; blank / Cybercab / - / Tooling; Nevada / Tesla Semi / - / Tooling; TBD / Roadster / - / Design development. Two thin light-grey horizontal rules separate the left-column sections. A faint wordmark is the only logo.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no gradients or shadows. Slide title in letter-spaced uppercase Calibri regular #333333. Section headings in bold Calibri #111111. Body paragraphs in regular Calibri #6E6E6E, roughly 2% of slide height, with generous line spacing. - text at x 4.5, y 48.9, w 44.9, h 19.5 [Calibri; font size 2.02; #6E6E6E]: We achieved our highest quarterly energy storage deployments, driven by record Megapack deployments. Total gross profit rose, both sequentially and year-over-year, to a record $1.1 billion, marking the fifth consecutive record quarter. We plan to begin Megapack 3 and Megablock production at Megafactory Houston in 2026. In 2025, our global Powerwall network supported more than 89,000 Virtual Power Plant events across over 1 million installed units, allowing homeowners to save over $1 billion in electricity bills as Virtual Power Plant participation continues to scale rapidly.

### Slide 9

**Text**

SUPPORTING INFRASTRUCTURE / AI Training Compute / We are currently building Cortex 2 at Gigafactory Texas to further increase our AI training compute capacity. In the first half of 2026, we plan to more than double the size of onsite compute in Texas (in terms of H100 equivalents). We aim to maximize capital efficiency by scaling training compute judiciously, including when the training backlog gets too long or in anticipation of greater demand from our engineers to support our AI-related offerings. / Battery / Our lithium refinery commenced pilot production and is the first spodumene to lithium hydroxide refinery in North America, leveraging a simpler, cheaper and more environmentally friendly process. This refinery enables us to domestically produce critical minerals in support of energy storage, battery manufacturing and ultimately for EV growth. / We have begun to produce battery packs for certain Model Ys with our 4680 cells, unlocking an additional vector of supply to help navigate increasingly complex supply chain challenges caused by trade barriers and tariff risks. We now produce dry-electrode for 4680 cells with both anode and cathode made in Austin. We expect both domestic cathode material in Texas and LFP lines in Nevada to begin production in 2026. / Other Supporting Infrastructure / We continue to efficiently utilize our existing physical footprint in North America, with targeted augmentation to support the rollout of Robotaxi. While in the short-term, operational workstreams such as charging, cleaning and maintenance can be managed through our existing charging network, service centers and sales and delivery locations, we will have to add more capacity as the service expands. We added over 3,800 net new Supercharging stalls, growing the network 19% year-over-year. / Installed Annual Capacity / Region / Product / Capacity / Status / AI Training Compute / Texas / Cortex 1 / >100k H100e / Production / Cortex 2 / - / Construction / Battery Manufacturing / Nevada / LFP / 7 GWh / Early Ramp / Texas / 4680 / 40 GWh / Production / Cathode Materials / 10 GWh / Early Ramp / Lithium Refining / 30 GWh / Early Ramp / Installed capacity ≠ current production rate and there may be limitations discovered as production rates approach capacity. Production rates depend on a variety of factors, including equipment uptime, component supply, downtime related to factory upgrades, regulatory considerations and other factors. Construction includes factory and infrastructure buildout as well as tool installation. / Existing Capacity / Future Planned Capacity / 300,000 / 250,000 / 200,000 / 150,000 / 100,000 / 50,000 / 0 / Jun-2021 / Sep-2021 / Dec-2021 / Mar-2022 / Jun-2022 / Sep-2022 / Dec-2022 / Mar-2023 / Jun-2023 / Sep-2023 / Dec-2023 / Mar-2024 / Jun-2024 / Sep-2024 / Dec-2024 / Mar-2025 / Jun-2025 / Sep-2025 / Dec-2025 / Mar-2026 / Jun-2026 / Tesla AI Training Capacity Ramp (H100 equivalent GPUs) / 9

**Layout**

Two columns below a letterspaced section title at top left (y about 9%). The left column (x 4.5 to 50) holds three bold-headed text sections separated by thin horizontal dividers at about y 37% and y 73%. The right column (x 54.6 to 95.6) holds a table with a bold caption above it, a small grey note under it, and a line chart with its axis title below. Left-aligned text, margins about 4.5% left and 4.4% right, page number at bottom left (y 95%) and a faint wordmark at bottom right.

**Visuals**

Table: titled Installed Annual Capacity (bold, centred above the table). Four columns: Region, Product, Capacity, Status. Header row in grey with white text. Group rows AI Training Compute (row 1) and Battery Manufacturing (row 4) are merged across all columns in bold. AI Training Compute rows: Texas / Cortex 1 / >100k H100e / Production; blank region / Cortex 2 / - / Construction. Battery Manufacturing rows: Nevada / LFP / 7 GWh / Early Ramp; Texas / 4680 / 40 GWh / Production; blank region / Cathode Materials / 10 GWh / Early Ramp; blank region / Lithium Refining / 30 GWh / Early Ramp. Product names bold; capacity values right aligned in Battery rows. Chart: line chart titled Tesla AI Training Capacity Ramp (H100 equivalent GPUs). X axis: time from Jun-2021 to Jun-2026, labels every three months, rotated about 45 degrees. Y axis: H100 equivalent GPUs, 0 to 300,000, gridlines every 50,000. Series 1 Existing Capacity: solid blue line, about 2,000 in Jun-2021 rising slowly to about 5,000 by Sep-2023, about 17,000 in Dec-2023, about 28,000 in Feb-2024, about 38,000 by Mar-2024 and flat to Sep-2024, about 88,000 by Dec-2024, about 107,000 by Apr-2025, about 120,000 by Jul-2025 and flat to about Nov-2025. Series 2 Future Planned Capacity: dotted blue line, about 140,000 from Jul-2025 to Jan-2026, then rising steeply to about 280,000 by Mar-2026 and flat to Jun-2026. Legend at top inside plot area: solid line Existing Capacity, dotted line Future Planned Capacity. No highlighted points. Values are estimates, none printed.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white slide with no ornament. Section title in letterspaced capitals, dark grey #333333. Bold subheads in near-black #111111. Body text in mid grey #6E6E6E, Calibri regular, about 2.0% of slide height with generous leading. Table header #7F7F7F with white text; table body rows #F2F2F2 with black #000000 text. Table note in light grey #BFBFBF, about 1.6% of height. Chart axis labels #595959, chart line blue. Chart title bold #7E7E7E. Thin light grey divider rules (#D9D9D9 approx.), about 1 px. No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 10

**Review notes**

- Review: The weaknesses are real, though: no page states a takeaway as a headline, footnotes and small table text are very faint and tiny, and slides 8, 10, 11 and 12 are dense paragraphs.

**Text**

A I & S O F T W A R E / AI Software / We continue to enhance FSD (Supervised)1 via our end-to-end foundation model trained on both customer and Robotaxi real-world data with our latest version, v14. FSD (Supervised)1 increasingly provides safety and convenience functionality that can relieve drivers of many tedious and potentially dangerous aspects of road travel, including giving access to personal transport for those who otherwise have difficulty driving. V14 offers unparalleled driver assistance to safely drive to the customer’s destination, find a free parking spot and park at that location. Our global fleet can collect the equivalent of over 500 years of continuous driving data per day2, allowing us to safely deploy and scale capabilities that can handle the long and fat tail of corner cases across diverse geographies and driving environments. / AI Inference Compute / Development of our in-house, custom designed AI5 and AI6 inference chips for autonomy progressed during the quarter, with production planned for 2027 and 2028, respectively. We are targeting a 50x improvement in performance for AI5 relative to AI4 thanks to 10x raw compute, 9x memory capacity and 5x hardened block quantization and softmax function (the latter enabling efficient low-precision computing without sacrificing model accuracy). / Automotive and Other Software / The Robotaxi iOS app no longer has a waitlist in the areas we serve. Our vehicles keep getting better with our over-the-air updates, including: Grok (an AI companion) which now supports navigation commands (allowing users to find, add and edit navigation destinations hands-free); Tesla Photobooth which enables users to take photos in their car and download or share via the Tesla mobile app; Supercharger Site Maps which displays Supercharger layouts, nearby businesses and live availability details; Automatic HOV Lane Routing based on interior camera occupancy detection; Phone Left Behind Chime and SpaceX ISS Docking Simulator Game. / 8.0 / 7.0 / 6.0 / 5.0 / 4.0 / 3.0 / 2.0 / 1.0 / 0.0 / FSD Miles on V12 and Beyond / FSD Miles on V11 and Before / Sep-2021 / Dec-2021 / Mar-2022 / Jun-2022 / Sep-2022 / Dec-2022 / Mar-2023 / Jun-2023 / Sep-2023 / Dec-2023 / Mar-2024 / Jun-2024 / Sep-2024 / Dec-2024 / Mar-2025 / Jun-2025 / Sep-2025 / Dec-2025 / Cumulative Miles Driven with FSD (Supervised)1 (billions) / Optimizing Tesla silicon / 5x / Hardened block quantization and softmax / 9x / Memory capacity / 10x / Raw compute increase / 50x / Total improvement over AI4 / Targeting Step-Function Improvement for our Next-Generation Inference Chip, AI5 / (1) Active driver supervision required; does not make the vehicle autonomous / (2) Calculated based on continuous hours of driving at an average of 30 miles per hour / 10

**Layout**

Single-slide white page with a small uppercase section label at top left, a two-column layout: left text column (about 4.6% to 50.8% of width) holding three bold headings with grey body paragraphs separated by thin grey horizontal rules; right column (about 55% to 96% width) holding an area chart at top, its caption, a dark image panel with its caption, and two footnotes at bottom. Left margin about 4.6%, right margin about 4%. Page number '10' bottom left at about 3% x and 95% y. Titles are left aligned; chart caption and image caption are centred under their graphics.

**Visuals**

Chart (top right): stacked-look cumulative area chart, two series, no chart title on the chart itself. X-axis: quarterly dates Sep-2021 to Dec-2025 (labels rotated about 45 degrees, every third quarter labelled, 18 labels). Y-axis: 0.0 to 8.0 in steps of 1.0, unit billions (caption says billions). Series 1 'FSD Miles on V12 and Beyond' in red #C00000, plotted as the upper area, rising from about 1.0 at Mar-2024 to about 2.0 at Dec-2024 to about 3.0 at Mar-2025 and about 7.3 at Dec-2025 (estimates; no values printed). Series 2 'FSD Miles on V11 and Before' in blue #4472C4, lower area, rising from about 0.0 at Sep-2021 to about 0.9 at Mar-2024 then flat at about 0.9 through Dec-2025 (estimates; no values printed). Legend centred at top of plot inside the chart area with red square then blue square markers. Gridlines light grey horizontal at each 1.0 step. No highlighted points. Image (bottom right, boxed, not cropped, not full-bleed): a flat black rectangle 800 by 450 pixels with a title 'Optimizing Tesla silicon' in white sans-serif at top left; a two-by-two grid of dark charcoal rounded-corner tiles (about #1C1C1C) on the left: top-left '5x' with 'Hardened block quantization and softmax', top-right '9x' with 'Memory capacity', bottom-left '10x' with 'Raw compute increase', bottom-right '50x' with 'Total improvement over AI4'; each big number in white with thin white divider line under it. Right of the tiles is a rendered three-quarter view of a dark AI processor package on a black background: a flat rectangular board with gold-edged border, several black heat-sink blocks on the right and a central green-gold die area with fine circuit texture; lit from upper left with soft highlights on the metal edges. Footnote markers and small faint grey glyph icons sit at bottom right corner (four tiny indistinct grey icons, about 91% to 97% x, 94% y), lightly faded.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background #FFFFFF. Section label 'A I & S O F T W A R E' in Calibri regular, uppercase, very wide letter-spacing, dark grey #333333. Headings in Calibri Bold, near-black #111111. Body in Calibri regular, mid grey #6E6E6E, with generous line spacing (about 1.5 lines). Chart caption and image caption in Calibri Bold grey #7E7E7E. Footnotes in Calibri regular light grey #BFBFBF. Accent colours: red #C00000 and blue #4472C4 in chart. Body text size about 2.0% of slide height (roughly 0.6 lines per 1% height ratio, cap height about 1.4%); headings the same size in bold; chart tick labels and footnotes about 1.6% of slide height. Thin horizontal divider rules in light grey #D9D9D9, about 0.1% of height thick, spanning the left column. No shadows or gradients on text; chart areas flat fills with no outline; image panel has square corners on its outer edge and slightly rounded tile corners.

### Slide 11

**Review notes**

- Review: The weaknesses are real, though: no page states a takeaway as a headline, footnotes and small table text are very faint and tiny, and slides 8, 10, 11 and 12 are dense paragraphs.

**Text**

SERVICES
Robotaxi
We began testing driverless Robotaxis in Austin in December and began removing the safety monitor from customer rides in January on a limited basis, which will unlock further expansion of our Robotaxi fleet and coverage area in the Austin-metro. Our Bay Area ride-hailing service began serving the San Jose Airport in October, with plans to expand to other major airports in the Bay Area upon receiving required permitting.
FSD (Supervised)1
We launched FSD (Supervised)1 in South Korea, where customers drove over 1 million kilometers using the software in just one month. While we continue to pursue regulatory approval in China and Europe, we began offering ride-along experiences to consumers in Italy, Germany, France and Switzerland.
Monthly subscriptions to FSD (Supervised)1 continued to grow sequentially and more than doubled in 2025. Starting this quarter, we are transitioning access to FSD (Supervised)1 to monthly subscriptions only as we begin to sunset the up-front payment option.
Automotive Services
Services and Other gross profit of approximately $300 million was partly driven by Part Sales and Supercharging. We now offer Tesla Insurance in Florida, as we continue to expand our insurance product to new states. In certain states, customers receive a discount on their insurance premiums when using FSD (Supervised)1. The more you drive with FSD (Supervised)1 enabled, the bigger the discount is on your insurance premium – helping, in certain cases, to completely offset the monthly subscription cost for FSD (Supervised)1.
Cumulative Paid Robotaxi Miles
State / Metro / Status
California / SF Bay Area / Safety Driver
Texas / Austin / Ramping Unsupervised
Dallas / 1H 2026
Houston / 1H 2026
Arizona / Phoenix / 1H 2026
Florida / Miami / 1H 2026
Orlando / 1H 2026
Tampa / 1H 2026
Nevada / Las Vegas / 1H 2026
Planned Robotaxi Coverage
(1) Active driver supervision required; does not make the vehicle autonomous.
11

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a white page: a left text column spanning about 4.6% to 51% of the slide width holds the Robotaxi, FSD (Supervised) and Automotive Services sections separated by thin horizontal rules, and a right column from about 54% to 96% holds the area chart with its caption above the table and the table's caption below it. The small uppercase title SERVICES sits top-left at about 9% of the height, with generous empty space above and between sections; the footnote and page number 11 sit bottom-left in the lower margin, and a faint Tesla wordmark sits bottom-right.

**Visuals**

Chart: area chart titled Cumulative Paid Robotaxi Miles (caption printed below the chart's x-axis region, bold grey). Y-axis 0 to 700,000 in steps of 100,000, with light grey horizontal gridlines at each step and labels 0, 100,000, 200,000, 300,000, 400,000, 500,000, 600,000, 700,000 printed right-aligned. X-axis categories Jun-2025, Jul-2025, Aug-2025, Sep-2025, Oct-2025, Nov-2025, Dec-2025, labels rotated about 45 degrees. Single series filled solid blue #4F81BD with no outline; no legend; no highlighted points. Values are not printed; estimated values: Jun-2025 about 0, Jul-2025 about 3,000, Aug-2025 about 20,000, Sep-2025 about 120,000, Oct-2025 about 265,000, Nov-2025 about 460,000, Dec-2025 about 655,000. Table: three columns State, Metro, Status with a grey header row (#7F7F7F, white text). Rows: California, SF Bay Area, Safety Driver; Texas, Austin, Ramping Unsupervised; blank state, Dallas, 1H 2026; blank state, Houston, 1H 2026; Arizona, Phoenix, 1H 2026; Florida, Miami, 1H 2026; blank state, Orlando, 1H 2026; blank state, Tampa, 1H 2026; Nevada, Las Vegas, 1H 2026. Body rows filled alternately with light grey #F2F2F2 cells and thin white separators; Metro names in bold, State and Status in regular. Logo: a faint letterspaced Tesla wordmark (T E S L A) in light grey #BFBFBF at the bottom right, about 91.5% to 97% of width and about 93% to 94% of height. No photographs or illustrations.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean white corporate financial-slide style. Titles and section headings are near-black #111111 bold sans-serif Calibri; the SERVICES title is regular Calibri in #333333 with very wide letter-spacing and all caps. Body text is Calibri regular in mid grey #6E6E6E at about 2.0% of slide height (line spacing roughly 1.5 lines). Chart and table captions are bold Calibri #7E7E7E at about 2.0% of height. Axis labels are small Calibri #595959 at about 1.6% of height. Table text is Calibri 2.0% in black #000000, with header text white. Thin horizontal grey divider rules (about #BFBFBF, 1 pt) separate the left-column sections. Footnote is Calibri at about 1.6% in #BFBFBF; page number is Calibri at about 1.6% in #BABABA. Accent colour is steel blue #4F81BD for the chart area. No corner radii, shadows or gradients; the table has square cells.

### Slide 12

**Review notes**

- Review: The weaknesses are real, though: no page states a takeaway as a headline, footnotes and small table text are very faint and tiny, and slides 8, 10, 11 and 12 are dense paragraphs.

**Text**

OTHER UPDATES
On January 16, 2026, Tesla entered into an agreement to invest approximately $2 billion to acquire shares of Series E Preferred Stock of xAI as part of their recent
publicly-disclosed financing round. Tesla’s investment was made on market terms consistent with those previously agreed to by other investors in the financing round. As
set forth in Master Plan Part IV, Tesla is building products and services that bring AI into the physical world. Meanwhile, xAI is developing leading digital AI products and
services, such as its large language model (Grok).
In that context, and as part of Tesla’s broader strategy under Master Plan Part IV, Tesla and xAI also entered into a framework agreement in connection with the
investment. Among other things, the framework agreement builds upon the existing relationship between Tesla and xAI by providing a framework for evaluating
potential AI collaborations between the companies. Together, the investment and the related framework agreement are intended to enhance Tesla’s ability to develop
and deploy AI products and services into the physical world at scale. This investment is subject to customary regulatory conditions with the expectation to close in
Q1'2026.
12

Single-column text slide with the section heading 'OTHER UPDATES' at top left, aligned to a left margin of about 4.6% of slide width, followed by two justified body paragraphs in a left-aligned text block about 79.5% wide; the slide number '12' sits bottom left and a faint wordmark sits bottom right, with the lower two-thirds of the slide left empty.

### Slide 13

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: The Outlook page uses short bold headings with one sentence each, a clear and restrained way to set expectations.

**Text**

OUTLOOK
Volume
We are focused on maximum capacity utilization at our factories. Deliveries and deployments will be impacted by aggregate demand for
our products, supply chain readiness and allocation decisions between sale to customers or use for our owned and operated fleet.
Cash
We will manage the businesses such that we ensure a strong balance sheet, maintaining sufficient liquidity to fund our product roadmap,
long-term capacity expansion plans – including further vertical integration – and other expenses.
Profit
While we continue to execute on innovations to reduce the cost of manufacturing and operations, over time, we expect our hardware-
related profits to be accompanied by an acceleration of AI, software and fleet-based profits.
Product
We continue to evolve and augment our product lineup with a focus on cost, scale and future monetization opportunities via services
powered by our AI software. We remain focused on growing our sales volumes through a differentiated and efficiently managed product
portfolio, which includes leveraging and optimizing our existing production capacity before building new factories and production lines.
Cybercab, Tesla Semi and Megapack 3 are on schedule for volume production starting in 2026. First generation production lines for
Optimus are being installed in anticipation of volume production.
13

**Layout**

Single-column text layout on a white page: a small letter-spaced section title OUTLOOK sits top-left at about 4.6% from the left edge and 8.9% from the top. Below it, four label and paragraph pairs run down the page; bold labels (Volume, Cash, Profit, Product) form a narrow left column starting at about 4.5% of the width, and the body paragraphs form a wide right column starting at about 18.9% of the width and ending near 83%, all left-aligned to the same top edges per pair. The lower part of the page is empty; the page number sits bottom-left at about 3% from the left and 95% from the top, and a faint wordmark sits bottom-right near 91% to 97% across.

**Visuals**

none

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with no decoration. Section title OUTLOOK in Calibri regular, widely letter-spaced capitals, dark grey #333333, about 2.0% of slide height, cap height about 1.4%. Labels in Calibri Bold, near-black #111111, about 2.0% of slide height. Photograph, boxed, not cropped at the frame edges, sitting inside the margins with square corners. Subject: a silver-grey SUV, shown from the rear three-quarter view, driving left to right along a curving two-lane asphalt road with white edge and centre lines. The car is in the centre-right of the frame, slightly below middle height. Its rear light bar glows red across the tailgate. The left side of the frame has a steep, tree-covered hillside with rocky cut face; the right side has rounded wooded hills and a calm lake. The background road and scenery show motion blur, implying speed. Sky is pale blue with thin white clouds. Lighting is soft natural daylight with a long shadow of the car falling on the road to the right. Dominant colours: silver-grey car, dark asphalt grey, muted olive and dark green foliage, pale blue sky, white lane markings, beige roadside verge. No overlay or tint. Also a faint light-grey wordmark at bottom right, about x 91 to 97%, y 92.5 to 93.5%, too small to read clearly.

The year and descriptor are mid grey #888888. Footnote and page number are very light grey #BFBFBF and #BABABA, about 1.6% of slide height, Calibri regular. The superscript (1) markers are about 1.05 to 1.35% of slide height. No shapes apart from the photograph, no rules, no shadows, no gradients, no corner radii. **Text**

FSD (SUPERVISED)1 – V14 OFFERS UNPARALLELED DRIVER ASSISTANCE / [photograph: no text on slide other than the screen display] / 17 / (1) Active driver supervision required; does not make the vehicle autonomous. / TESLA

**Layout**

A single full-width photograph is boxed below a one-line title. The title sits top-left at about 8.9% from the top with a left margin of about 4.6%, and the photo spans from x 4.5% to 95.5% and from y 12.1% to 92.7%. The footer row at the bottom holds the page number at left (x 3.0%), the footnote at x 5.9% and a faint Tesla wordmark at bottom right; there is no other grid or column structure.

**Visuals**

One photograph, full colour, in a sharp-cornered rectangle (no crop beyond the frame edges, no overlay or tint). Subject: the interior of a Tesla car seen from the driver's seat. A black three-spoke-style steering wheel with a Tesla emblem on the hub is at left. Centre console touchscreen is tilted toward the viewer, showing a dark navigation map with a blue route line curving down the centre, small grey building footprints and a yellow warning triangle on the route. The screen shows on its left panel a blue button labelled 'Start Self-Driving' and partial 'Open Frunk' and 'Open Trunk' buttons; a white card reads '0.3 mi' with 'Ben Joaquin Rd', and a second card reads 'Mountain Village' with '0.8 mi'; a bottom bar reads '6:10 pm', '25 min', '9.8 mi', 'Work', 'End Trip'. A person's arm in a dark sleeve reaches from the bottom centre and the index finger points at the screen. Through the windshield is a stone-and-wood building: a warm lit wooden front door at left, a stone wall, a wide wooden-plank staircase in the centre and a dark wall light at right. A rear-view mirror is at the top centre. A thin red ambient light strip runs along the dashboard edge. Lighting is soft and natural with warm amber light from the building and cool daylight through the glass. Dominant colours: near-black #1A1A1A for the dash and cabin, warm beige stone #C8B89A, tan wood #8A6A45, and red #D0103A for the ambient strip. Composition: eye-level, slightly off-centre, shallow depth of field with the screen and hand in focus.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background with no decoration. Title in Calibri Bold, all capitals, wide letter-spacing, about 2.0% of slide height; 'FSD (SUPERVISED)' in dark grey #333333 and 'V14 OFFERS UNPARALLELED DRIVER ASSISTANCE' in mid grey #888888, with a superscript 1 in #6E6E6E and an en dash in black. Footer text is Calibri regular about 1.6% of slide height in light grey #BFBFBF; the page number is #BABABA. Photo has square corners, no border and no shadow. No accent colours, gradients or icons apart from the photograph itself.

### Slide 22

**Text**

GIGAFACTORY SHANGHAI – 9 MILLIONTH VEHICLE PRODUCED (GLOBALLY) / 22

**Layout**

Single full-width photograph below a left-aligned letter-spaced header line at top-left; the photo spans nearly the full slide width with equal side margins of about 4.5%, and a small page number sits at bottom-left with a faint logo wordmark at bottom-right. No grid of columns; the image dominates the frame.

**Visuals**

Photograph of a group of about two hundred Tesla factory workers posing in the Gigafactory Shanghai production hall to mark the 9 millionth vehicle. Workers in black jackets, navy and yellow hi-vis vests, and caps (some red, light blue and white) crowd around a white Tesla Model Y parked at centre-right, with silver foil balloons spelling '9', '0', '0' (shown as 900 in the photo) in front of the car. Many workers are raising their hands and waving. Behind them is the automated production line with robotic arms, machinery, and several black vehicles on the line; a long conveyor rack runs along the right. The composition is a wide aerial/elevated view, full-bleed within a rectangular box with no border, no crop of the subject, lit by even overhead factory light; dominant colours are grey concrete floor, white, black and silver. No overlay or tint.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White #FFFFFF background with no decoration. Header text in Calibri Bold, all capitals, letter-spaced widely, about 2.0% of slide height; the first part in dark grey #333333 and the rest in mid grey #888888. Page number in Calibri regular about 1.6% of slide height in light grey #BABABA. Faint small logo wordmark at bottom-right in very light grey. Photograph is a plain rectangle with square corners and no shadow.

### Slide 23

**Text**

GIGAFACTORY NEVADA – 6 MILLIONTH DRIVE UNIT PRODUCED / 23

**Layout**

A single full-width photograph sits in a boxed area spanning about 4.5% to 95.5% of the slide width and 12.1% to 93% of its height, below a one-line uppercase title left-aligned at the top-left margin. The title is set in letter-spaced caps above the image; the page number sits bottom-left below the image, and a faint wordmark sits bottom-right. There is no body text and no footer band.

**Visuals**

Photograph, not cropped, full frame within a rectangular box (2786 by 1472 px). Subject: about 150 to 200 employees in dark work clothing (black t-shirts, black hoodies, caps, jeans, work boots) stand shoulder to shoulder in a factory aisle, facing the camera from an elevated viewpoint. In the foreground, a line of people holds up large gold foil balloon numerals that together read 6000000, spanning roughly 28% to 80% of the image width, with the first numeral 6 at left and the last 0 at right; the balloons are glossy metallic gold. Centred in the lower foreground on a wooden pallet is a silver drive unit (gearbox assembly). On the left, stacked brown cardboard boxes, a red plastic bin and a grey-lidded container; on the right, boxes printed IMC 100 in black and yellow, and a small glass jar on a grey surface. In the background are large white Tesla machine enclosures with red accents and Tesla wordmarks, labels such as DLI2-1C400-Q1 and 36G and 36H on white panels, red safety railings, and a conveyor on the right. Lighting is bright, even overhead factory light with no strong shadows; dominant colours are white and light grey machinery, gold balloons, black clothing and red accents. No overlay or tint.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white slide background #FFFFFF with no gradient or pattern. Title text is bold uppercase Calibri-style sans-serif, widely letter-spaced, about 2.0% of slide height; the first phrase GIGAFACTORY NEVADA – is dark grey #333333 and the phrase 6 MILLIONTH DRIVE UNIT PRODUCED is mid grey #888888. Page number is Calibri regular, about 1.6% of slide height, light grey #BABABA. Photo is a sharp rectangle with square corners, no border, no shadow. Wordmark is light grey, widely spaced capitals, no border.

(1) Beginning in Q1'25, Capital expenditures is presented inclusive of purchases of energy generation and storage systems and all prior periods have been adjusted.
(2) As a result of the adoption of the new crypto assets standard, the previously reported quarterly periods in 2024 have been recast.
(3) Beginning in Q1'25, Adjusted EBITDA (non-GAAP) is presented net of digital assets gains and losses and all prior periods have been adjusted.
25

**Layout**

Three equal-width bar charts sit side by side in three columns across the middle of the slide, each with its own y-axis at the left of its plot and quarter labels rotated beneath. The title block is top-left at about 4.6% from the left edge and 9% from the top, with the subtitle beneath it; the footnotes run left-aligned in three lines along the bottom, starting at about 6% from the left, with the page number at the bottom-left margin and a faint wordmark at the bottom-right.

**Visuals**

Three clustered column charts with no chart titles beyond their axis or legend labels. Chart 1: Vehicle Deliveries (millions of units), single blue series, y-axis 0.0 to 2.0 in steps of 0.2, x-axis 1Q-2023 to 4Q-2025; values are not printed, so about 1Q-2023 1.42, 2Q-2023 1.63, 3Q-2023 1.72, 4Q-2023 1.80, 1Q-2024 1.77, 2Q-2024 1.74, 3Q-2024 1.77, 4Q-2024 1.79, 1Q-2025 1.73, 2Q-2025 1.68, 3Q-2025 1.71, 4Q-2025 1.63. Chart 2: y-axis 0 to 18 in steps of 2, no unit label on axis; blue series Operating Cash Flow ($B) with about values 13.2, 13.9, 12.1, 13.2, 11.0, 11.5, 14.4, 14.9, 16.8, 15.7, 15.6, 14.7; red series Free Cash Flow ($B) with about values 5.8, 6.1, 3.7, 4.3, 1.4, 1.7, 3.6, 3.6, 6.7, 5.5, 6.8, 6.2; legend below the chart with blue and red squares. Chart 3: y-axis 0 to 20 in steps of 2; blue series Net Income ($B) with about values 11.7, 12.1, 10.7, 14.9, 13.8, 12.5, 12.9, 7.1, 6.1, 5.9, 5.0, 3.7; red series Adjusted EBITDA ($B) with about values 18.6, 19.3, 18.1, 16.6, 15.8, 14.8, 15.7, 16.0, 15.5, 15.2, 14.7, 14.6; legend below with blue and red squares. No highlighted points. Bars are vertical, grouped in pairs per quarter in charts 2 and 3, single bar per quarter in chart 1; bars show a slight vertical shading gradient, darker at their edges. Horizontal light grey gridlines at each y-axis tick.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. White background with no decoration. Title KEY METRICS in Calibri Bold, widely letter-spaced capitals, colour #333333, and TRAILING 12 MONTHS (TTM) in Calibri Bold, widely letter-spaced capitals, colour #888888, both about 2.0% of slide height, cap height about 1.4%. Subtitle (Unaudited) in Calibri Regular #333333 at about 2.0%. Chart tick labels Calibri Regular #595959 at about 2.0%. Legend and axis titles Calibri Regular #7F7F7F at about 2.0%. Footnotes Calibri Regular #BFBFBF at about 1.6%, superscript markers at about 1.05%. Page number Calibri Regular #BABABA at about 1.6%. Blue bars #3A6EA5 with darker #2F5C8F edges; red bars #A83232 with darker #8E2A2A edges; legend squares blue #3A6EA5 and red #B03A3A. Gridlines #D9D9D9, 0.75 pt. No shadows, no rounded corners, no axis lines beyond gridlines.

### Slide 26

**Text**

KEY METRICS YEAR–OVER–YEAR FINANCIAL SUMMARY
Revenue
Total quarterly revenue decreased 3% YoY to $24.9B. YoY, revenue was impacted by the following items(1):
- decrease in vehicle deliveries
- lower regulatory credit revenue
+ growth in Energy Generation and Storage
+ growth in Services and Other
+ positive FX impact of $0.3B1
+ growth in other automotive ancillary sales, partly driven by an increase in FSD subscriptions
+ higher vehicle average selling price (ASP) (excl. FX impact1), inclusive of mix impact
Profitability
Our quarterly operating income decreased 11% YoY to $1.4B, resulting in a 5.7% operating margin. YoY, operating income was primarily
impacted by the following items(1):
- increase in SBC and Restructuring and Other charges
- increase in operating expenses (excl. SBC and Restructuring and Other) driven by AI and other R&D projects and SG&A
- higher average cost per vehicle due to lower fixed cost absorption for certain models and an increase in tariffs
- decrease in vehicle deliveries
- lower regulatory credit revenue
+ higher vehicle average gross profit due to mix and pricing impacts
+ growth in Energy Generation and Storage gross profit
+ growth in Services and Other gross profit
+ growth in other automotive ancillary sales, partly driven by an increase in FSD subscriptions
Cash
Quarter-end cash, cash equivalents and investments was $44.1B. The sequential increase of $2.4B was primarily the result of positive free cash
flow.
26
(1) Impact is calculated on a constant currency basis. Actuals are compared against current results converted into USD using average exchange rates from Q4’24.

**Layout**

Single-column text slide with a two-column structure: a narrow left label column (x about 4.5% to 15%) holding the bold section labels Revenue, Profitability and Cash, and a wide right content column starting at x 19.2% and running to about x 87%. The letter-spaced section heading sits top-left at y 8.9%, left-aligned to x 4.6%. Paragraph labels align to the top of their content blocks. Bullet lines are stacked with even spacing and no grid or columns beyond these two. A small footnote sits bottom-left at y 95% with the page number at the far left, and a faint logo sits bottom-right; there are no borders or dividers.

**Visuals**

none

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background (#FFFFFF) with no ornament, gradients or shadows. Heading 'KEY METRICS' in bold Calibri-style sans-serif, dark grey #333333, all caps, wide letter-spacing, about 2.0% of slide height. Subtitle 'YEAR–OVER–YEAR FINANCIAL SUMMARY' in bold Calibri-style sans-serif, mid grey #888888, all caps, wide letter-spacing, same size. Section labels Revenue, Profitability and Cash in bold Calibri-style sans-serif, near-black #111111, about 2.0% of slide height. Intro paragraphs in regular Calibri-style sans-serif, grey #6E6E6E, about 2.0% of slide height. Bullet lines in regular Calibri-style sans-serif, black #000000, about 2.0% of slide height, with line pitch about 2.9% of slide height. Footnote text in regular Calibri-style sans-serif, light grey #BFBFBF, about 1.6% of slide height; page number #BABABA. Body text is roughly 2% of slide height, so headings and body are almost the same size and differ mainly in weight and colour. No rules, boxes, or rounded corners.

**Layout**

A single full-width financial reconciliation table on a plain white slide. The title block sits top-left at about 9% from the top, left-aligned to a margin of about 4.6% of slide width, with the table running from about 14.7% to 80% of the height and spanning about 4.6% to 95.5% horizontally. The column headers are right-aligned over five numeric columns, with the Q4-2025 column last and highlighted. Three footnotes sit bottom-left below the table from about 93% to 98% of the height, and the slide number 31 sits at the bottom-left margin at about 95% height. A faint wordmark sits bottom-right.

**Visuals**

A single data table with a header row and 23 body rows. Columns: row label (left-aligned, about 4.6% to 51% of width), then Q4-2024, Q1-2025, Q2-2025, Q3-2025, Q4-2025 (right-aligned numbers). Rows in order: header 'In millions of USD or shares as applicable, except per share data' with the five period headers; then the row values as listed in text. Bold rows are the GAAP and non-GAAP subtotal rows: Net income attributable to common stockholders (GAAP) 2,128 / 409 / 1,172 / 1,373 / 840; Net income attributable to common stockholders (non-GAAP) 2,107 / 934 / 1,393 / 1,770 / 1,761; Net income used in computing diluted EPS 2,104 / 934 / 1,393 / 1,770 / 1,761; EPS diluted GAAP 0.60 / 0.12 / 0.33 / 0.39 / 0.24; EPS diluted non-GAAP 0.60 / 0.27 / 0.40 / 0.50 / 0.50; Adjusted EBITDA 4,333 / 2,814 / 3,401 / 4,227 / 4,154; Adjusted EBITDA margin 16.9% / 14.6% / 15.1% / 15.0% / 16.7%; Automotive gross margin (GAAP) 16.6% / 16.2% / 17.2% / 17.0% / 20.4%; Automotive gross margin excluding regulatory credit sales 13.6% / 12.5% / 15.0% / 15.4% / 17.9%. **Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white slide with no decoration. Title in spaced uppercase sans-serif (Calibri regular, letter-spaced about 0.3 em) in dark grey #333333, with '(Unaudited)' beneath in the same style at smaller size. **Design**

**Layout**

A single full-width table page with no separate title block beyond a tracked-caps heading at top left; content is left-aligned at about 4.6% margin, with the 15-column numeric tables right-aligned to a common right edge near 94%. Four data tables are stacked vertically with a blank gap between groups; footnotes sit at the bottom left above a faint Tesla wordmark at bottom right, and the page number 33 sits at bottom left margin.

**Visuals**

Four tables with no chart or image. Table 1 (quarterly, 15 columns 2Q-2022 to 4Q-2025, rows: Net cash provided by operating activities (GAAP); Capital expenditures (1); Free cash flow (non-GAAP) (1)). Table 2 (quarterly, 15 columns, rows: Net income attributable to common stockholders (GAAP) (2); Interest expense; Provision for (benefit from) income taxes (2); Depreciation, amortization and impairment; Stock-based compensation expense; Digital assets loss (gain), net (2); Adjusted EBITDA (non-GAAP) (2) (3)). Table 3 (trailing twelve months, 12 columns 1Q-2023 to 4Q-2025, rows: Net cash provided by operating activities – TTM (GAAP); Capital expenditures – TTM (1); Free cash flow – TTM (non-GAAP) (1)). Table 4 (TTM, 12 columns, rows: Net income attributable to common stockholders – TTM (GAAP) (2); Interest expense – TTM; Provision for (benefit from) income taxes – TTM (2); Depreciation, amortization and impairment – TTM; Stock-based compensation expense – TTM; Digital assets loss (gain), net – TTM (2); Adjusted EBITDA – TTM (non-GAAP) (2) (3)). Two light grey (#F2F2F2) spacer bands separate the row groups in tables 1 and 3. Thin light grey horizontal rules sit under each header and row. Faint Tesla wordmark at bottom right, rendered in very light grey.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white slide with no decoration. Heading in widely letter-spaced uppercase sans-serif (Calibri-like, regular weight) in dark grey #333333, about 2.0% of slide height for the heading and 1.7% for the body and table text. Table numbers and labels are black #000000 regular Calibri-like sans-serif. Footnotes and page number are small light grey text (estimated #A6A6A6). Spacer bands #F2F2F2. Hairline table rules in light grey. No shadows, corner radii or gradients.

### Slide 34

**Text**

ADDITIONAL INFORMATION
WEBCAST INFORMATION
Tesla will provide a live webcast of its fourth quarter 2025 financial results conference call beginning at 4:30 p.m. CT on January 28, 2026 at ir.tesla.com. This webcast will also be available for replay for approximately one year thereafter.
CERTAIN TERMS
When used in this update, certain terms have the following meanings. Our vehicle deliveries include only vehicles that have been transferred to end customers with all paperwork correctly completed. Our energy product deployment volume includes both customer units when installed and equipment sales at time of delivery. “Net income attributable to common stockholders (non-GAAP)” is equal to (i) net income attributable to common stockholders before (ii)(a) stock-based compensation expense, net of tax, (b) digital assets (gain) loss, net of tax and (c) release of valuation allowance on deferred tax assets. “Adjusted EBITDA (non-GAAP)” is equal to (i) net income attributable to common stockholders before (ii)(a) interest expense, (b) provision for (benefit from) income taxes, (c) depreciation, amortization and impairment, (d) stock-based compensation expense and (e) digital assets loss (gain), net. “Free cash flow” is operating cash flow less capital expenditures. Average cost per vehicle is cost of automotive sales divided by new vehicle deliveries (excluding operating leases). “Days sales outstanding” is equal to (i) average accounts receivable, net for the period divided by (ii) total revenues and multiplied by (iii) the number of days in the period. “Days payable outstanding” is equal to (i) average accounts payable for the period divided by (ii) total cost of revenues and multiplied by (iii) the number of days in the period. “Days of supply” is calculated by dividing new car ending inventory by the relevant period's deliveries and using trading days. Constant currency impacts are calculated by comparing actuals against current results converted into USD using average exchange rates from the prior period.
NON-GAAP FINANCIAL INFORMATION
Consolidated financial information has been presented in accordance with GAAP as well as on a non-GAAP basis to supplement our consolidated financial results. Our non-GAAP financial measures include non-GAAP net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders, non-GAAP net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders on a diluted per share basis (calculated using weighted average shares for GAAP diluted net income (loss) attributable to common stockholders), Adjusted EBITDA, Adjusted EBITDA margin, non-GAAP automotive gross margin and free cash flow. These non-GAAP financial measures also facilitate management’s internal comparisons to Tesla’s historical performance as well as comparisons to the operating results of other companies. Management believes that it is useful to supplement its GAAP financial statements with this non-GAAP information because management uses such information internally for its operating, budgeting and financial planning purposes. Management also believes that presentation of the non-GAAP financial measures provides useful information to our investors regarding our financial condition and results of operations, so that investors can see through the eyes of Tesla management regarding important financial metrics that Tesla uses to run the business and allowing investors to better understand Tesla’s performance. Non-GAAP information is not prepared under a comprehensive set of accounting rules and therefore, should only be read in conjunction with financial information reported under U.S. GAAP when understanding Tesla’s operating performance. A reconciliation between GAAP and non-GAAP financial information is provided above.
FORWARD-LOOKING STATEMENTS
Certain statements in this update, including, but not limited to, statements in the “Outlook” section; statements relating to the development, strategy, ramp, production and capacity, demand and market growth, cost, pricing and profitability, investment, deliveries, deployment, availability and other features and improvements and timing of existing and future Tesla products and services and supporting infrastructure; statements regarding operating margin, operating profits, spending and liquidity; and statements regarding expansion, improvements and/or ramp and related timing at our facilities are “forward-looking statements” within the meaning of the Private Securities Litigation Reform Act of 1995. Forward-looking statements are based on assumptions and management’s current expectations, involve certain risks and uncertainties, and are not guarantees. Future results may differ materially from those expressed in any forward-looking statement. The following important factors, without limitation, could cause actual results to differ materially from those in the forward-looking statements: the risk of delays in launching and/or manufacturing our products, services and features cost-effectively; our ability to build and/or grow our products and services, sales, delivery, installation, servicing and charging capabilities and effectively manage this growth; our ability to successfully and timely develop, introduce and scale, as well as our consumers’ demand for, products and services based on artificial intelligence, robotics and automation, electric vehicles, advanced driver assistance systems, and ride-hailing services generally and our vehicles and services specifically; the ability of suppliers to deliver components according to schedules, prices, quality and volumes acceptable to us, and our ability to manage such components effectively; any issues with lithium-ion cells or other components manufactured at our factories; our ability to ramp our factories in accordance with our plans; our ability to procure supply of battery cells, including through our own manufacturing; risks relating to international operations and expansion, including unfavorable and uncertain regulatory, political, economic, tax, tariff, export controls and labor conditions; any failures by Tesla products to perform as expected or if product recalls occur; the risk of product liability claims; competition in the automotive, transportation and energy product and services and robotics markets; our ability to maintain public credibility and confidence in our long-term business prospects; our ability to manage risks relating to our various product financing programs; the status of government and economic incentives for electric vehicles and energy products; our ability to attract, hire and retain key employees and qualified personnel; our ability to maintain the security of our information and production and product systems; our compliance with various regulations and laws applicable to our operations and products, which may evolve from time to time; risks relating to our indebtedness and financing strategies; and adverse foreign exchange movements. More information on potential factors that could affect our financial results is included from time to time in our Securities and Exchange Commission filings and reports, including the risks identified under the section captioned “Risk Factors” in our annual report on Form 10-K filed with the SEC on January 30, 2025 and subsequent quarterly reports on Form 10-Q. Tesla disclaims any obligation to update information contained in these forward-looking statements whether as a result of new information, future events or otherwise.
